### Verified updates after the table

- **Texture/Image multi-style TySh correction (#482):** The Texture/Image row additionally includes exact original-text rendering for 1–64 decoded UTF-16 style runs through explicit per-FontSet project TTF/OTF/WOFF assets, with per-run size, tracking, RGBA, deterministic authored faux bold/italic, common baseline layout, masks, hashes, and strict leased Inspector/MCP evidence. Real Photoshop terminal run units are narrowly normalized and reported. Remaining text gaps are complex-script shaping, vertical/warped text, and broader character/paragraph styles; the Texture/Image row remains Partial.
- **Texture/Image smart-object replacement correction (#474):** The Texture/Image row additionally includes exact top-level embedded `liFD` replacement into separate no-overwrite project PSD-v1 copies. The File Inspector and two strict shared MCP tools use one byte-exact leased `bounded-smart-object-payload-replacement-v1` backend. Recursive nested-payload editing and live smart-filter/transform/warp rendering remain partial.
- **Texture/Image recursive smart-object replacement correction (#475):** The same File Inspector and strict shared MCP workflow now selects non-overlapping exact 1–8-level embedded `liFD` paths, rebuilds every containing PSD-v1 document through `bounded-recursive-smart-object-payload-replacement-v1`, and retains top-level request compatibility. Live smart-filter/transform/warp rendering and semantic layer-level editing inside embedded documents remain partial.

- **Material importer correction (#362):** Material importer settings are no longer passive metadata. `.material` and `.mtl` assets are processed even when build optimization is disabled; standard Babylon/PBR and Node Material texture objects share bounded project-path validation and deterministic embedded PNG/JPEG extraction, while MTL map directives are parsed without mistaking directive options for texture paths. Node Materials compile through a real NullEngine/Scene build and return compiler errors, disconnected-input warnings, block/texture/output counts, and compiled-shader evidence. Editor and CLI builds publish portable `.bjsmaterial.json` reports, include extracted textures in asset manifests on both fresh and cached builds, and regenerate missing generated files. Exact source/settings SHA-256 leases guard atomic preview artifacts. The File Inspector displays validity, reference/missing/extracted counts, compile state, diagnostics, and the shared Apply action. `get_material_importer_result` and `apply_material_importer` expose the same workflow to Codex CLI and Claude CLI. This closes the executed-importer gap but does not make the broader Materials row Unity-complete.
- **Animation importer correction (#363):** Animation importer settings are no longer passive metadata. `.animation` AnimationGroups and legacy `.animations` arrays are bounded, parsed through Babylon Animation semantics, resampled at the requested 1–240 FPS while preserving duration, optionally reduced against the full sampled key set, assigned the requested loop mode, and checked for a resolvable position/Y-rotation root-motion target. `.animator` and `.controller` documents preserve their authored content while validating bounded, uniquely named states and transition endpoints. A rejected middle track cannot misassociate later target metadata. Editor/CLI builds publish processed assets plus portable `.bjsanimation.json` evidence and repair missing cached sidecars; exact source/settings SHA-256 leases guard atomic preview artifacts. The File Inspector displays source, sampled, and output key counts, clip/track timing, root motion, controller statistics, diagnostics, and Apply. `get_animation_importer_result` and `apply_animation_importer` expose the same workflow to Codex CLI and Claude CLI. This closes the executed-importer gap but does not make the broader Animation or Animator rows Unity-complete.
- **Prefab correction (#344):** Prefab variants no longer freeze a creation-time clone. Version-2 metadata records the exact recursively resolved base revision and stable source-node overrides; nested chains dynamically inherit current base content, while cycle/depth/duplicate/property validation and missing/ambiguous target conflicts prevent silent corruption. Exact revision leases guard persisted rebase and Prefab Mode writes. Conflicted overrides can be removed in the editor or completely replaced/retargeted through shared MCP. `.prefab` double-click opens a real isolated Babylon scene with hierarchy selection, transform/name/visibility editing, stale-base status, Rebase, and confirmed save; the active game scene remains untouched. Instantiation consumes the same resolved document and refuses conflicts. Four dedicated shared tools bring the prefab surface to thirteen schemas. Automated NullEngine and full-suite verification plus a live Electron/port-3712/CDP scenario prove dynamic inheritance, persisted override editing, visible scene instantiation, stale-base rebase, and Prefab Mode save/readback. This supersedes the Scene and Prefabs rows' earlier missing dynamic-rebase and prefab-mode statements.
- **Prefab property correction (#345):** Existing JSON-serialized node properties can now be inspected and overridden through bounded RFC 6901 paths on base prefabs or variants. Exact resolved-revision leases, JSON/value/path/count bounds, stable source-node targeting, duplicate rejection, and protected prototype/identity/hierarchy/standard-property roots prevent unsafe or stale writes. Recursive composition applies property overrides after standard overrides, preserves them across base changes and nested variants, and emits explicit missing-property conflicts. Prefab Mode provides JSON inspect/edit/apply/reset with inherited-versus-overridden state. Two dedicated tools bring the prefab surface to fifteen schemas; automated and live Electron/CDP/MCP verification proves base write, variant persistence, UI apply, and reset-to-inherited behavior. Structural overrides and component add/remove remain incomplete.
- **Prefab structure correction (#346):** Stable-source structural override sets now remove complete subtrees, add dependency-ordered empty transform nodes or shallow mesh clones, and reparent existing or newly added nodes without allowing them outside the prefab root. Deterministic added identities, exact leases, count/shape bounds, root protection, target/parent/source validation, and cycle detection make composition and repair atomic. Recursive variants inherit the resolved structure before applying ordinary and arbitrary property overrides. Prefab Mode exposes Add Child, Remove Node, and Reparent; two dedicated tools bring the surface to seventeen schemas. Automated NullEngine and live Electron/CDP/MCP verification proves composed hierarchy instantiation and UI add/remove/reparent persistence. Component add/remove and nested child-prefab references remain incomplete.
- **Prefab composition correction (#347):** Variants can now add and remove bounded serialized metadata components and recursively reference contained child-prefab assets. Safe component keys, exact revision leases, protected runtime identity fields, bounded JSON, and stricter known scripts/physics shapes guard mutations. Nested composition namespaces stable identities, remaps serialized resource references, preserves dependency propagation, detects missing assets and cycles, and supports later subtree/component/property overrides inside the nested content. Prefab Mode exposes both authoring workflows; two dedicated shared tools bring the prefab surface to nineteen schemas. Thirteen focused tests, the complete runtime/editor gates, real 674-tool stdio discovery, and live Electron/CDP/MCP component add/reset plus three-level nested instantiation verify the implementation. Instance unpacking and selective Apply/Revert at nested-prefab boundaries remain incomplete.
- **Prefab instance-boundary correction (#348):** Instantiated nodes now carry bounded ordered source provenance from the outer variant through each nested child asset. The Mesh Inspector and five new shared tools inspect exact boundary revisions, Apply/Revert transform/visibility and selected components against a chosen base or variant source, promote transform/property/component/descendant-structure overrides into a nested source with two-file rollback protection, unpack only the outer boundary while preserving deeper links, or unpack completely while retaining live objects and hierarchy. Fifteen focused tests, full 153/379 runtime/editor gates, MCP/editor builds, 679-tool stdio discovery, and a live Electron/CDP/MCP promotion plus two-stage unpack scenario verify the workflow. Automatic capture of arbitrary manually introduced live structural differences and multi-instance override comparison remain incomplete.

- **Project collaboration:** Versioned project-local changelists, identity-aware diff/merge, exact conflict values, and reusable rules cover local workflows. Opt-in enforcement adds hashed one-time credentials, admin/editor/viewer members, expiring presence, key rotation/session invalidation, final-admin protection, per-stdio-client tokens, and central viewer read-only enforcement. An explicitly enabled remote HTTP/HTTPS gateway runs the same authorized editor endpoint map and broadcasts local/remote operations plus rich presence through a bounded replayable SSE ring. Presence includes stable colors/tools, primary/hover/selection state, bounded multi-viewport mouse/pen/touch/XR pointers with optional world rays/hits, backward-compatible cursors, and shared camera framing. Its sanitized event journal survives editor restarts with configurable bounded retention, filtered/gap-aware pagination, monotonic sequence checkpointing, corruption quarantine, and explicit administrator clearing. Independent revision-aware channels protect transforms, common node state, hierarchy parents, and exact safe property-path sets. Project scripts, shaders, JSON, markup, styles, Markdown, and text support stable-ID RGA collaboration with deterministic concurrent inserts, tombstone deletes, idempotent operations, divergence blocking, and hash-guarded external-file rebasing. Named ordered JSON scene collections support deterministic anchored inserts, stable-ID update/move/tombstone operations, pagination, idempotent retries, divergence blocking, and hash-guarded rebasing. Dedicated remote lock routes share the atomic lease store and stream sanitized lock events. Project-contained development TLS certificate generation supports bounded SAN/validity/RSA configuration, secure private-key permissions, inspection, expiry and key-match diagnostics, explicit rotation, and review-first trust guidance without changing the operating-system trust store. Opt-in LAN discovery uses bounded multicast/local UDP query-response, persistent random public identity, short-lived nonce-matched announcements, sanitized labels, gateway endpoints, authentication flags, and optional TLS fingerprints while excluding paths, allowlists, members, sessions, tokens, and key/certificate contents. A NAT-friendly outbound WSS relay client tunnels action, presence, event-read, and lock traffic through the same central RBAC with registration timeout, heartbeat, frame/concurrency bounds, exponential reconnect, live/environment-only credentials, and credential-safe status. The repository includes a deployable managed relay server with per-project registration secrets, public collaboration-bearer HTTP forwarding, health, timeouts, concurrency/frame/rate limits, and single-editor replacement. Project-scoped Git staging/unstaging, confirmed commits, and confirmed non-force pushes now share the same role enforcement. The Scene Inspector and shared MCP expose these workflows. Hosted relay deployment operations remain outside the editor; this update supersedes earlier statements that remote Git writes, managed relay, discovery, certificate automation, rich presence, durable event history, text and ordered-list CRDT editing, and the completed collaboration layers were absent.
- **Source-control correction (#333–343):** The table's earlier read-only Git description is superseded. The editor and shared MCP now provide exact project-scoped status/history/staged-or-worktree diff, selective/all stage and unstage, confirmed commits and non-force pushes, bounded local/remote branch and tag inventory, confirmed fetch/prune/tag refresh, clean-root fast-forward-only pull, local branch create/switch/safe-delete, lightweight or annotated local tag create/delete, fully local credential-safe fetch/push authentication diagnostics, environment-credential GitHub, GitLab, Bitbucket Cloud, and Azure DevOps review-provider configuration/list/detail/create/approve/comment/request-changes where supported plus exact-head-SHA leased merge/squash/rebase where supported, reviewer/team/label inspection and fingerprint-leased replacement where supported, normalized GitHub check-runs/Actions, GitLab pipelines/jobs, Bitbucket commit statuses, or Azure PR statuses/policy evaluations, and exact-head guarded reruns or policy requeue where the provider exposes them, merge-base/divergence/file-impact preview, review-first no-auto-commit merge, non-interactive rebase, bounded unmerged-stage inspection, ours/theirs/delete/externally-edited conflict resolution, 256-KiB Base/Ours/Theirs/current-worktree text previews, safe binary/oversized metadata, SHA-256 stage fingerprints, rollback-safe custom UTF-8 resolution up to 1 MiB, bounded non-animated PNG/JPEG/WebP Base/Ours/Theirs previews and RGBA statistics, exact same-dimension changed-pixel/error/bounds metrics plus heatmaps, continue/abort, authoritative remote heads/tags/default-branch inspection, non-force branch/tag publication, and exact-hash leased remote deletion with default-branch protection. Hosted-review credentials remain environment-only; redirects are disabled, responses/timeouts bounded, errors redacted, mutations admin/confirmation guarded, and merge or rerun re-inspects the provider head before applying the caller's exact SHA lease. Bitbucket reviewer mutation uses inspected immutable `{UUID}` values and explicitly rejects unsupported teams, labels, status reruns, and rebase. Azure uses GUID reviewers, supports labels but not teams, maps decisions to reviewer votes and comments to threads, supports PAT Basic or Bearer mode, and requeues only inspected policy-evaluation GUIDs. Authentication diagnostics classify transports, generic plus URL-scoped helpers, helper persistence/security/executable availability, AskPass/SSH overrides, and SSH-agent socket readiness without contacting the remote, invoking a credential provider, or returning URLs, usernames, commands, paths, environment values, or secrets. Image conflict inspection rejects or metadata-bounds absent, unsupported, animated, malformed, byte/pixel-oversized, and dimension-mismatched stages and never writes original or preview data to disk. Git runs without a shell or interactive prompting; contained paths, symlink rejection, stale-stage blocking, nested-project index isolation, complete-worktree cleanliness for repository-wide changes, current-branch-only pull, collaboration editor/admin staging roles, admin-only repository administration, ref/remote validation, credential redaction, and embedded-credential rejection are enforced. Automatic semantic binary merges, non-raster binary viewers, and editor-managed credential-helper configuration remain missing.
- **Rendering volumes:** Persisted axis-aligned volumes now support authored edge blend distance and per-volume weight. Overlapping profiles compose deterministically from low to high priority against the active camera's captured baseline in preview and exported runtime; the Scene Inspector and shared MCP expose the same values. Render graph and arbitrary shader-pass authoring remain missing.
- **Custom render passes:** A persisted render graph now authors arbitrary GLSL shader passes, explicit fixed-function copy passes, real offscreen scene-raster passes, and native WebGPU WGSL compute passes. Raster passes draw selected or all current scene meshes from an authored/active camera into one named `RenderTargetTexture`; compute passes dispatch authored workgroups into an owned RGBA storage texture after graph raster targets and before camera post-processes. Compute settings include entry point, output binding, direct/indirect dispatch, once/every-frame mode, readable project/raster/compute textures, structured uniform buffers, and typed storage buffers. Named outputs flow into downstream shader/copy/compute consumers. Typed live storage-buffer ranges and uniform fields can be mutated without rebuilding; bounded storage ranges support asynchronous GPU readback. Graph-wide shared storage keys reuse one allocation, declare read/write access, reject unordered RAW/WAR/WAW hazards, and report WebGPU's real implicit command-order synchronization. Selected passes can add an explicit command-submission boundary. A draggable typed Compute Graph panel persists nodes and connections and generates complete WGSL; its 34-node library includes vector math, interpolation, clamping, legacy numeric selection, scalar/vector split/combine/splat conversions, swizzles, typed comparison/Boolean/branch control, texture/uniform/storage reads, storage writes, and output writes. Reusable project-local graph-function assets expand selected pure fragments into editable prefixed node instances with typed boundaries. Instances retain asset version/SHA-256 revision metadata, render as collapsible call nodes, diagnose missing/outdated/incompatible dependencies, and safely refresh compatible external connections by stable interface name. Version-1 assets load through a deterministic semantic migration that preserves their interface while replacing numeric selection with typed comparison/branch control; the editor and MCP provide dry-run diagnostics and backup-safe version-2 persistence. Dedicated shared MCP covers graph/node/edge CRUD, compilation, subgraph asset CRUD/insertion/migration, instance lifecycle/diagnostics, representative CPU previews, bounded project-texture thumbnails, graph debugging, and execution profiling. Texture thumbnails expose decoded dimensions, hashes, color extrema/averages, alpha coverage, and optional PNG data in the editor and MCP. Named shader, copy, scene-raster, compute-storage, and MRT outputs support bounded live readback previews with byte/float normalization, orientation control, non-finite diagnostics, and the same pixel statistics. Single-output shader/copy passes own stable public render textures and forward their captured pixels back into the normal screen chain, avoiding private transient post-process texture access. Hardware timestamp profiling reports isolated shader/copy/raster/MRT/compute GPU durations with bounded histories, explicit capability reasons, and WebGL round-robin sampling when independent timestamp queries are available. Shader passes also support scalar/vector uniforms, depth, geometry-buffer normals, project textures, and up to four real MRT attachments. Preview and exported runtime use the same validated schedule and owned resource lifecycle; WebGL preserves compute authoring while reporting that WebGPU compute support is required. Shared MCP exposes CRUD, rebuilding, schedules, pass/shared-resource settings, live/authored buffer workflows, output capture, isolated GPU profiling, diagnostics, backend warnings, and compiler/dispatch errors. Native WebGPU raster/compute timestamp execution and real MRT primary/secondary attachment readback through downstream copy/shader consumers are verified.
- **Compute graph node library:** The typed compute graph has expanded to 34 node types. In addition to math, interpolation, conditional selection, and typed storage operations, it now supports scalar constants, vector split/combine/splat conversion, four-component swizzles, six component-wise comparisons, Boolean-vector logic, and typed component-wise branching. Shared graph/node/edge MCP tools expose every type and constrained authored property to Codex and Claude through the same schema. This verified update supersedes the Rendering table row's earlier statement that richer compute nodes and scalar/vector conversions were absent.
- **Compute subgraph assets:** Selected pure compute fragments can be saved as validated version-2 `.computegraph.json` graph-function assets with named typed boundary inputs and one typed output. The editor and shared MCP can list, inspect, insert, migrate, and delete them; insertion expands collision-free editable nodes and returns ports for normal graph wiring. Tracked instances add collapsible call-node visualization, SHA-256 revision diagnostics, safe compatible refresh, missing/invalid dependency reporting, group movement, and owned-node cleanup. Legacy version-1 assets receive a deterministic dry-run-capable migration with content-addressed backups and stable interfaces. This verified update supersedes the Rendering table row's earlier statement that compute subgraphs, call visualization, and asset migrations were absent.
- **Compute graph debugging:** All 34 compute-node types support deterministic representative CPU previews with typed value propagation and declared side effects. Static diagnostics expose disconnected inputs and dead nodes; combined debug output reports compiler order/WGSL readiness, live target state, dispatch counts, per-pass CPU submission duration, and available whole-frame GPU timing. The editor panel and shared MCP tools expose the same data without mislabeling a frame-level GPU value as an isolated pass duration.
- **Compute texture thumbnails:** Project-backed texture-load nodes can display bounded decoded thumbnails in the Compute Graph panel and return the same PNG/pixel diagnostics through shared MCP. Requests enforce project realpath containment, node/dimension limits, optional metadata-only responses, and deterministic nearest/bilinear resampling. Texture-backed named scene-raster, compute, and MRT outputs can also be read and previewed live; native WebGPU MRT device capture and stable single-output post-process ownership are verified.
- **Web build:** A Web build profile can configure and generate a project-local `manifest.webmanifest` and a cache-first, offline-fallback service worker from persisted `settings.pwa` metadata through the Scene Inspector or shared MCP. The editor exposes manifest metadata, output path, service-worker path, precache URL paths, and fallback URL. It can idempotently install/update worker registration in a standard root `index.html`; framework projects without one receive an actionable manual-registration error instead of unsafe source changes. Template-specific framework registration and icon-entry editing UI remain incomplete.
- **Performance budgets:** Persisted diagnostic threshold sets now evaluate max frame time, draw calls, mesh/resource counts, vertices, particles, and available GPU timing through the Scene Inspector and shared MCP. Each run records per-metric pass/fail/unavailable status; unavailable backend metrics are never fabricated. This is a lightweight scene-performance gate, not a full Unity Performance Test framework.
- **Audio diagnostics:** The Scene Inspector and shared MCP now report every SoundNode's loaded/playing state, 2D/3D mode, base/effective volume, mixer-bus influence, and stale bus-node assignments. This is runtime state inspection, not a replacement for a full waveform/mixer-effects profiler.
- **Device tooling:** When Device Simulator is active, the preview now exposes a visible hold-and-drag touch surface with normalized coordinates and an active touch indicator. It invokes the same guarded Input Actions simulation path as `simulate_input_touch`; an actionable error is shown if preview runtime input is inactive. Remote physical-device deployment, logs, and mobile signing remain missing.
- **Animator:** 2D blend trees now include a persisted **Freeform Directional** mode. It weights clips by both heading alignment and child/input magnitude (for example, walk versus sprint), runs identically in preview/exported runtime, is selectable in the Animator panel, and is accepted by the shared animator MCP tools. Humanoid avatars and retargeting remain missing.
- **Animation curves:** Scalar timeline tracks now render draggable curve points. Vertical drags change real key values with Undo/Redo and synchronize the key inspector; `set_animation_curve_keys` provides the same bounded scalar-key replacement workflow to both MCP clients. Vector/color multi-channel curve-canvas editing remains missing.
- **Animator:** Root motion is now supported for generic animation: a configured animated source node contributes position and optional Y-rotation deltas to a separate character target in preview and exported runtime. The Animator panel and `set_animator_root_motion` MCP tool expose the same persisted configuration. Humanoid avatars and retargeting remain missing.
- **Rigging:** Two-bone IK now exposes an optional pole-target selector in the Skeleton Inspector and allows MCP mutation through `set_ik_controller`; the live controller is safely recreated only when the pole target changes. Constraint-rig graphs and humanoid retargeting remain missing.
- **Rigging / Avatar correction (#366):** The editor now persists None/Generic/Humanoid Avatar definitions with 55 canonical human roles, deterministic auto-mapping, complete manual Skeleton Inspector mapping, required/optional/duplicate/unknown/hierarchy validation, captured rest poses, human scale, and T-pose arm evidence. Valid source/target Avatars can bake rest-pose-corrected rotation and height-scaled root-translation tracks into ordinary editable AnimationGroups. Model Importer rig-type evidence, exported runtime lookup/validation, and seven shared MCP tools are included. This supersedes earlier statements that humanoid Avatar mapping and retargeting were absent. Rigging remains **Partial** versus Unity because muscle-limit authoring, full humanoid body-part Avatar Masks, Optimize Game Object/exposed-transform workflows, constraint-rig graphs, advanced IK, and production retarget-debug visualization remain incomplete.
- **Humanoid muscles / Avatar Mask correction (#367):** Mapped humanoid roles now support optional local XYZ degree limits relative to the captured rest pose, enforced in preview and exported runtime and authored from the Skeleton Inspector or shared MCP. Reusable Avatar Masks toggle nine Unity-style body groups plus additional named transform branches, are authored beside the Avatar, and can be selected by Animator base states or weighted layers while legacy generic target-name masks remain compatible. This supersedes #366's statement that muscle-limit authoring and humanoid body-part masks were absent. Rigging remains **Partial** for Unity-normalized muscle-space visualization and pose preview, Optimize Game Object/exposed transforms, constraint-rig graphs, advanced IK, and production retarget debugging.
- **Optimize Game Object correction (#368):** Generic and Humanoid model imports now provide `Optimize Game Object` plus bounded `Extra Transforms to Expose` paths. The shared processor copies current linked-node transforms into bones, unlinks the skeleton, retargets AnimationGroup and direct node tracks to Bone properties, removes skeleton-only Transform nodes deepest-first, flattens retained exposure proxies to the model anchor, and automatically retains any linked node that owns a non-skeleton attachment. Serialized exposure metadata is resolved in editor preview and generated runtime so requested sockets, script references, and IK targets follow their internal bones after animation evaluation. The Model Inspector reports candidates, removed/exposed transforms, retargeted tracks, requested/automatic/missing exposures, and diagnostics. `set_model_rig_optimization`, `get_model_importer_result`, and `apply_model_importer` expose the complete leased workflow to Codex CLI and Claude CLI. Rigging remains **Partial** for normalized muscle-space visualization/pose preview, production retarget debugging, constraint-rig graphs, advanced IK, skin-weight authoring, and per-model clip configuration.
- **Humanoid Muscles & Settings correction (#369):** Humanoid pose inspection now converts mapped local rotation deltas into normalized -1…1 XYZ muscle values using each role's authored limits or explicit ±180° fallback. The Skeleton Inspector shows normalized sliders, degree readback, limit source/violations, active/mapped counts, Rest Pose, deterministic horizontal arm-chain T-pose enforcement, selected-role reset, and exact Stop & Restore. Preview sessions capture the initial Bone or linked TransformNode rotations, reapply after animation evaluation, remain outside persisted Avatar and animation data, and are stopped before scene serialization. Three shared MCP tools inspect, set, and stop this same temporary pose workflow. This closes #367/#368's normalized muscle visualization/pose-preview gap; Rigging remains **Partial** for production retarget debugging, constraint-rig graphs, advanced IK, skin-weight authoring, and per-model clip configuration.
- **Humanoid retarget debugger correction (#370):** `inspectHumanoidRetarget` now performs a bounded dry run across all 55 canonical roles before baking. It reports source/target Avatar validation, mapping/rest-pose availability, track properties, recognized/compatible/skipped totals, per-role skip reasons, correction Euler/angle evidence, human-scale ratio, root-motion and scale inclusion, and whether baking is safe. The Skeleton Inspector exposes source Avatar/clip selection, root/scale toggles, compact role diagnostics, guarded bake, and temporary cyan/magenta SkeletonViewer overlays with optional local axes. Overlays are non-serializable, idempotently updated/disposed, and removed before scene save. `inspect_humanoid_retarget` and `set_humanoid_retarget_debug_visualization` provide the same external workflow. This closes #369's production retarget-debug visualization gap; Rigging remains **Partial** for constraint-rig graphs, advanced IK, skin-weight authoring, and per-model clip configuration.
- **Animation Rig layers correction (#371):** Scene metadata now persists skeleton-bound rig layers with explicit integer order, enablement, 0…1 weight, and ordered constraints. One shared post-animation evaluator runs in preview and exported runtime. Multi-parent constraints capture each source's target-relative position and rotation offsets, blend up to eight weighted sources, and apply the layer×constraint influence in bone world space. Twist constraints capture source/distributed-bone rest rotations, extract signed swing-twist around a normalized local axis, and distribute weighted rotation to up to sixteen bones. The Skeleton Inspector authors layer lifecycle/order/weight and creates/toggles/reweights/removes both constraint types. Project load now also restores the previously persisted look-at constraints. Seven strict MCP tools expose the complete layer/constraint lifecycle. This closes #370's basic production constraint-rig ordering and multi-parent/twist gap; Rigging remains **Partial** for chain/full-body IK, advanced aim/multi-position constraints, skin-weight authoring, per-model clip configuration, and a freeform constraint canvas.
- **Chain IK correction (#372):** Ordered rig layers now include a Unity-style `chainIk` constraint. Authoring validates a bound skeleton mesh, a root that is an ancestor of the tip, a 2–64-bone chain with non-zero consecutive segments, and a TransformNode target. The shared post-animation runtime uses bounded FABRIK with 1–64 iterations and 0.0001–100 cm tolerance, fully extends unreachable chains, blends the solve through layer×constraint×chain-rotation weight, and optionally matches target world rotation at the tip. The Skeleton Inspector creates the constraint and edits target, iteration, tolerance, chain rotation, and tip rotation while showing bone count, reachability, and tip error. Existing strict `list_rig_layers`, `create_rig_constraint`, and `set_rig_constraint` MCP tools expose the same lifecycle and diagnostics. This closes #371's single-chain IK gap; Rigging remains **Partial** for full-body IK, advanced aim/multi-position constraints, skin-weight authoring, per-model clip configuration, and a freeform constraint canvas.
- **Multi-Position / Multi-Aim correction (#373):** Ordered rig layers now include `multiPosition` and `multiAim`. Both validate and blend 1–8 non-zero weighted TransformNode sources. Multi-Position captures optional per-source local position offsets, blends desired world positions, and independently masks X/Y/Z without disturbing locked channels. Multi-Aim captures an optional rotation offset, validates non-zero/non-parallel local aim/up axes, constructs local/world orthonormal bases, stabilizes roll against a configurable world-up axis, and falls back safely when target direction and world up are parallel. The Skeleton Inspector creates both constraints, edits offset/masks/axis presets, and displays source counts plus position/angle error. Existing strict rig list/create/set MCP tools expose all seven weighted-constraint fields and live diagnostics. This closes #372's Multi-Position/Multi-Aim gap; Rigging remains **Partial** for full-body IK, remaining override/damped/blend constraints, skin-weight authoring, per-model clip configuration, and a freeform constraint canvas.
- **Full-Body IK correction (#374):** Ordered rig layers now include `fullBodyIk`: one root plus 1–8 unique descendant effectors, each targeting a TransformNode with independent 0…1 position and rotation weights. The shared post-animation solver uses bounded CCD iterations across shared and branch joints, solves toward weight-blended positions, stops at centimeter tolerance, and applies optional target world rotation after positional convergence. Authoring rejects missing/duplicate/root effectors, invalid ancestry, zero-length consecutive path segments, missing targets, zero total effector influence, and out-of-range solver settings. The Inspector creates the solver, manages effectors and weights, edits iterations/tolerance, and reports path size, chain length, reachability, current error, reached count, average error, and maximum error. Existing strict rig list/create/set tools expose the same nested effector contract. This closes #373's multi-effector IK gap; Rigging remains **Partial** for override/damped/blend constraints, skin-weight authoring, per-model clip configuration, and a freeform constraint canvas.
- **Override/Damped/Blend correction (#375):** Ordered rig layers now include `overrideTransform`, `dampedTransform`, and `blendTransform`. Override captures one source's target-relative position and rotation offsets; Damped adds persistent frame-rate-aware exponential smoothing with independently configurable position and rotation damping; Blend interpolates between two distinct offset-aware source targets. All three support independent position/rotation weights and world XYZ masks, reject unusable all-disabled channels, and report live position/rotation errors. The Skeleton Inspector creates and edits all three types. Existing strict rig list/create/set tools expose the complete source, weight, mask, damping, and blend contract. This closes #374's transform-constraint gap; Rigging remains **Partial** for skin-weight authoring, per-model clip configuration, and a freeform constraint canvas.
- **Skin-weight authoring correction (#376):** Skinned meshes now support one to eight named-bone influences per vertex across Babylon main and extra matrix buffers. Read-only inspection returns paginated vertices, local positions, named influences, normalized sums, a complete validation report, and an exact SHA-256 edit lease. Replacement and painting support explicit vertices or a spherical local-space brush, replace/add/subtract/topology-smooth modes, opacity/falloff, proportional redistribution, normalization, pruning, repair, limiting, and fallback binding. Mirroring searches opposite-side geometry within tolerance and remaps common or explicit Left/Right bone pairs. The Skeleton Inspector consumes Mesh Modeling vertex selections and records exact Undo/Redo snapshots. Save/export explicitly restores extra buffers omitted by Babylon's serializer before binary geometry output, while unsafe changed-count topology edits are rejected. Five strict MCP tools expose the complete workflow. This closes #375's skin-authoring gap; Rigging remains **Partial** for per-model clip configuration and a freeform constraint canvas.
- **Per-model animation clip correction (#377):** Model importer settings now persist up to 64 strict clip definitions selecting a source AnimationGroup, inclusive source range, output name, Loop Time/Loop Pose behavior, exact target-name/id mask, root-motion node, position mode, and rotation-Y extraction. The shared processor validates every definition before mutation, evaluates missing boundary keys, slices and rebases tracks to frame zero, optionally closes the loop pose, preserves additive/playback metadata, emits bounded source/output/root-motion evidence, and disposes source groups only after atomic success. The Model Inspector provides complete authoring and diagnostics. Editor preview and CLI builds now load model asset containers so AnimationGroups, skeletons, materials, textures, meshes, and transforms remain available together, including a corrected Babylon data-URL path. `get_model_animation_clips` and `set_model_animation_clips` use strict schemas and exact importer fingerprints, while the existing apply/result tools execute and verify the same build. This closes #376's per-model clip gap; Rigging remains **Partial** for a freeform constraint-node canvas, while broader Animation/Animator parity remains **Partial** for Unity-depth controller interoperability, curve editing, and runtime debugging.
- **Freeform rig constraint graph correction (#378):** Every persisted Animation Rig layer now exposes a draggable SVG data-flow canvas in the Skeleton Inspector. It derives bounded Transform/bone input nodes, typed constraint nodes, driven-bone outputs, and labeled dependency edges for Multi-Parent, Twist, Chain IK, Multi-Position, Multi-Aim, Full-Body IK, Override, Damped, and Blend constraints. Freeform positions persist inside the exported rig-layer metadata without affecting deterministic runtime evaluation; malformed legacy positions fall back safely, invalid references are visibly marked, and deterministic source/constraint/output auto-layout remains available. `get_rig_constraint_graph` returns topology, positions, bounds, validity, and an exact SHA-256 lease; `set_rig_constraint_graph_layout` atomically patches/replaces positions or auto-layouts up to 512 existing nodes and rejects stale, duplicate, unknown, non-finite, negative, or oversized coordinates. This closes #377's visual constraint-canvas gap. Rigging remains **Partial** only for deeper Unity Animation Rigging package extensibility, custom Animation Jobs/constraints, baking, and profiling; broader Animation/Animator gaps remain tracked separately.
- **Animator layer, override, IK Pass, recursive Blend Tree, and conditional Entry correction (#385/#388/#389/#390/#391/#392/#393):** Override and Additive layer modes, normalized additive reference poses, Base/earlier-layer synchronization, qualified state mapping, per-source Motion/behaviour overrides, recursively nested 1D/2D/Direct Blend Trees, independent duplicate leaves, child Time Scale/Cycle Offset/Mirror, ordered conditional Entry routing, child-machine-source Exit routing, shared normalized clip time, weight-based Affects Timing, and Base/additional-layer IK Pass execution are implemented in the Animator panel, editor preview, exported runtime, debugger, Unity YAML import, and focused/shared MCP. Additive and modified child clips are internal non-serialized clones and do not mutate authored AnimationGroups; synchronized layers inherit source transitions and Entry routing, reject independent Entry/play controls, and can replace Motion or behaviours without losing source-state lifecycle identity. Direct trees map one numeric parameter to each child and optionally normalize active weights. With Affects Timing disabled, the source duration is the sole master and target clips stretch to it; with it enabled, the target duration contributes according to layer weight. IK Pass calls typed target-node `onAnimatorIK` scripts after sampling and before existing rig observers, with bounded per-layer evidence. Animator remains **Partial** for deeper Unity controller/MonoBehaviour import interoperability, automatic Unity clip/AvatarMask binding, controller-integrated muscle tracing, transition history, breakpoints, and frame stepping.
- **Profiling:** The Scene Inspector and MCP can record bounded timestamped diagnostics captures and report per-metric minimum, maximum, and average values. CPU/GPU call-stack timelines, memory snapshots, and loading analysis remain missing.
- **Device tooling:** The Scene Inspector and MCP configure a persisted device simulation profile that resizes the actual preview engine view for portrait/landscape resolution, DPI, and safe areas. Remote device logs, physical-device deployment, and mobile signing remain missing.
- **Mobile builds:** Android and iOS build profiles export the active scene then run a project-provided `build:android` or `build:ios` package script with the normal `BJS_EDITOR_*` settings environment. First-party SDK integration, signing, store packaging, and device deployment remain missing.

## Sequential implementation rule

Asset-pipeline correction (#351): the earlier Asset pipeline table row is superseded for import state/errors, tags, and favorites. Version-3 sidecars and the persistent registry now keep bounded tags separate from labels, project-local favorites, and native/unchecked/current/stale/missing/error source health with safe fingerprints and durable reimport diagnostics. The Assets Browser and File Inspector expose indicators, filters, editing, and explicit source checks; four shared MCP tools plus extended registry filters expose the same behavior. Type-specific Unity-equivalent importers, other binary dependency formats, semantic GUID reference remapping, worker-scale indexing, and a visual dependency graph remain incomplete, so overall Asset pipeline parity remains Partial.

Asset-importer correction (#352): the editor now has a shared version-1 importer contract with typed defaults, validation, legacy migration, presets, and File Inspector controls for texture, model, audio, video, font, material, animation, and custom assets. Four shared MCP tools expose discovery, inspection, validation, and batch application. Editor and CLI builds honor `includeInBuild`; texture export honors maximum size, resampling algorithm, and mipmap generation; preview model import honors unit conversion, scale, material/animation inclusion, and collider generation; importer changes invalidate asset output caches. Audio, video, font, material, and animation now have executed conversion, generation, validation, or compilation consumers; several advanced model/texture options remain validated and persisted without complete consumers. This closes the importer-interface gap, not full Unity Asset Pipeline parity, which remains Partial.

Asset-dependency correction (#353): registry version 5 adds bounded dependency scanners for Babylon/glTF JSON, OBJ/MTL/DAE text, GLB JSON chunks, ASCII/binary FBX string properties, and 3DS texture-map chunks. Every indexed source records complete, deferred, malformed, or not-applicable status plus an actionable message; malformed and oversized inputs remain diagnostic instead of producing guessed edges. The File Inspector and shared dependency graph/diagnostic MCP tools expose the same evidence, while a new scanner-discovery tool lists formats and byte limits. Registry migration now prunes dependency/build/cache/generated trees, excludes metadata sidecars, prevents recursive sidecar generation, and supports direct-folder queries so nested results cannot starve Assets Browser pagination. Compressed/archive containers, semantic GUID remapping, worker-process indexing, and the visual graph canvas remain incomplete, so overall Asset pipeline parity remains Partial.

Semantic asset-move correction (#354): asset and folder rename/move now use the persistent dependency index to produce a non-mutating exact-fingerprint plan, then apply one GUID-preserving transaction across the asset, sidecar, and supported referencers. Structured/editor/source text, OBJ/MTL/DAE directives, CSS URLs, and validated GLB JSON strings preserve root-relative, relative, slash, query, and fragment spelling; JSON keys and prefix lookalikes are not modified. Relative references that remain valid when their containing folder moves are recognized without false blockers. FBX/3DS and malformed/oversized/semantically unmatched sources block explicitly unless the caller knowingly overrides them. Failure at any write restores already-written references, metadata, and the original asset path. The Assets Browser confirms the same plan, while `inspect_asset_move`, `apply_asset_move`, and upgraded `move_asset` expose it to Codex CLI and Claude CLI. Worker-process indexing is added by #355; a visual dependency graph, compressed/archive scanning, and safe opaque-binary rewriting remain incomplete, so overall Asset pipeline parity remains Partial.

Background asset-indexing correction (#355): complete and incremental registry scans now send bounded file stat, SHA-256 hashing, and text/GLB/FBX/3DS dependency parsing to up to eight isolated workers—Electron node-enabled Web Workers in the live renderer and `worker_threads` in Node—while the authoritative renderer path retains stable sidecar metadata reconciliation and atomic registry publication. Non-blocking rebuild/refresh jobs report queued/discovering/analyzing/metadata/publishing/complete phases, processed/total files, worker count, result counts, timestamps, actionable failures, and a bounded 20-job history. Only one job can run; refresh accepts 1–100 contained paths; cancellation terminates workers before publication and leaves the previous registry authoritative. The Assets Browser shows progress and provides start/cancel controls. Three shared MCP tools expose status, start, and exact-job cancellation to Codex CLI and Claude CLI. A visual dependency graph, compressed/archive formats, opaque-binary rewriting, and additional codec consumers remain incomplete, so overall Asset pipeline parity remains Partial.

Visual asset-dependency correction (#356): the persistent dependency model now returns deterministic traversal depth, visible canonical cycles, and per-edge cyclic evidence in addition to GUID/type/missing/truncation data. A dedicated zoomable SVG canvas opens from the Assets Browser, every File Inspector, or MCP; it supports Uses/Used By traversal, depth and missing controls, path highlighting, node selection, rerooting, Inspector navigation, missing/cycle summaries, and explicit edge-limit warnings. `get_asset_dependency_graph` exposes the enriched evidence, `export_asset_dependency_graph` produces bounded JSON, Graphviz DOT, or Mermaid text without writing files, and `open_asset_dependency_graph` opens the same live canvas for Codex CLI and Claude CLI. Compressed/archive formats, opaque-binary rewriting, and additional codec consumers remain incomplete, so overall Asset pipeline parity remains Partial.

Compound-archive dependency correction (#358): registry version 6 safely indexes ZIP, TAR, TAR/GZip, and `.unitypackage` compound assets up to bounded compressed, expanded, member-count, per-entry, and aggregate scan limits. It rejects traversal/duplicate member paths, reports malformed or deferred archives without aborting registry publication, records member inventory and internal/project/missing references, remaps Unity-package GUID folders to logical `assets/...` paths, resolves Unity YAML GUID references, and exposes archive-local cycles and missing nodes through virtual dependency-graph nodes. The File Inspector lists archive contents and missing internal references, while the existing scanner, dependency, diagnostics, graph, export, and open-graph MCP tools expose the same evidence to Codex CLI and Claude CLI. Focused registry coverage passes 17 tests, the complete editor gate passes 411 tests across 118 files, production builds and focused lint pass, stdio discovery reports the updated schemas among 705 tools, and a live Electron scenario displayed three archive members, five references, one missing member, and one cycle across six graph nodes/eight edges. Additional codec consumers and safe semantic rewrites inside archives remain incomplete, so overall Asset pipeline parity remains Partial.

Archive semantic-move correction (#394): exact GUID-preserving asset moves now rebuild bounded ZIP, TAR, TAR/GZip, and Unity-package referencers when their indexed text or GLB members contain project-external references to the moved asset. Only registry-proven external member edges are considered, so archive-internal relative links remain unchanged. Plans expose archive format and rewritten-member counts; malformed/encrypted/oversized/unsafe archives, unsupported TAR member types, and archived FBX/3DS reference strings become explicit blockers. ZIP comments, timestamps, attributes, directory entries and member contents are preserved; TAR metadata, directory entries, checksums, and Unity-package GUID storage paths are preserved. Rebuilt archives participate in the existing atomic write/rollback transaction. The same `inspect_asset_move`, `apply_asset_move`, and `move_asset` workflows are strict closed-world MCP tools for Codex CLI and Claude CLI. Opaque binary reference rewriting and additional per-format codec consumers remain incomplete, so overall Asset pipeline parity remains Partial.

Binary model semantic-move correction (#395): ASCII FBX strings, pre-7500 32-bit and 7500+ 64-bit binary FBX string-property tables, and recursive 3DS texture-name chunks now support safe length-changing reference rewrites. FBX node/property counts, string sizes, hierarchy depth, property types, absolute end offsets, ancestor offsets, null terminators, and untouched footer bytes are validated and preserved; 3DS child chunks are recursively rebuilt with corrected parent lengths and preserved suffix bytes. The same rewriters execute inside ZIP/TAR/TAR-GZip/Unity-package members, where only registry-proven project-external values may change and colliding archive-internal relative links remain untouched. Post-rewrite size limits, malformed stale-source blockers, exact fingerprints, and multi-file rollback remain enforced. Existing strict semantic-move MCP tools expose the evidence to Codex CLI and Claude CLI. Additional per-format codec consumers and dependency/rewrite support for other opaque binary formats remain incomplete, so overall Asset pipeline parity remains Partial.

Texture-importer correction (#396): PNG, JPEG, BMP, WebP, GIF, TIFF, and SVG importer settings are no longer passive metadata. One bounded Sharp-based processor executes orientation, aspect-safe resize, selectable resampling, alpha removal, deterministic output encoding, quality mip variants, optional CPU-readable RGBA output, and effective sampling color space in the Inspector, editor build, and CLI build. Exact source/settings fingerprints lease atomic project-local previews; builds publish portable runtime sidecars and repair incomplete cached artifacts; generated runtime redirects authored URLs without discarding selected KTX2 payloads. `get_texture_importer_result` and confirmation-gated `apply_texture_importer` expose the same result and mutation workflow to Codex CLI and Claude CLI. Later #420/#421/#422 corrections add bounded TGA, merged-composite PSD, and HDR/OpenEXR/cubemap execution; #423 adds executed Web/Desktop overrides across Inspector, editor/CLI builds, cache identity, runtime evidence, and strict MCP; #424 adds deterministic rotated atlas packing plus Sprite Manager/Sprite Map runtime consumption. The subsequent #425 correction adds bounded layered PSD pixel extraction, primary-mask alpha application, Inspector authoring, atomic PNG publication, and strict MCP. The subsequent #426 correction adds dependency-triggered and optional external-origin Auto Reimport across every executed importer family, Inspector controls, persistent job evidence, and strict MCP. The subsequent #427 correction adds exact-coordinate clipping-group composition through 16 separable Photoshop blend modes with Inspector and strict MCP evidence; #428 adds exact Hue/Saturation/Color/Luminosity blending; #429 adds bounded legacy `lrFX` Solid Fill metadata and RGB effect baking; #430 adds bounded legacy Inner Shadow/Inner Glow metadata and deterministic in-bounds baking; #431 adds bounded legacy Drop Shadow/Outer Glow metadata and safe bounds-expanding baking; #432 completes legacy `lrFX` Bevel metadata and deterministic dual-mode edge baking; #433 adds bounded modern `lfx2` descriptor parsing and solid-color inside/center/outside Stroke baking, while correcting legacy style 5 to named stroke emboss; #434 adds executable aligned, non-dithered classic solid-gradient Strokes across all five styles with exact stops/settings; #435 adds exact noise-gradient metadata plus deterministic bounded seeded RGB/HSB/HSL/Lab Stroke execution; #436 completes solid-gradient alignment/dithering/interpolation; #437 resolves Patt/Pat2/Pat3 tiles and executes Pattern Strokes/Overlays; #438 executes percent-opacity RGB Color Overlays in descriptor order; #439 executes solid/noise Gradient Overlays through the shared bounded gradient models; #440 adds real `lmfx` decoding and executable zero-choke Drop/Inner Shadows. Modern glows/bevel/satin, nonzero-choke/contour shadow rendering, and remaining `lmfx` families remain incomplete, so overall Texture/Image parity remains Partial.

PSD layered-compositing correction (#449–#470): the shared bounded layered-PSD path now executes primary/real-user/vector adjustment masks per pixel plus Color Balance, Black & White, Channel Mixer, Selective Color, solid/noise Gradient Map, Photo Filter, embedded CUBE/3DL Color Lookup, Brightness/Contrast, Levels, Invert, Posterize, Threshold, piecewise-linear Curves, linear-sRGB Exposure, bounded HSL Vibrance, and Photoshop-4/Photoshop-5+ Hue/Saturation master/colorize/normalized feathered local ranges. Real `lsct`/`lsdk` section-divider blend keys and bottom-marker/children/top-marker ordering drive exact pass-through-group adjustment propagation. Explicit opt-in isolated groups recursively composite supported raster and isolated-group children up to 32 levels, including clipping, child and group effects, correctly scoped internal/external adjustments, blend modes, child/group opacity, primary/real-user/vector group masks through `bounded-group-mask-v1`, and union bounds through `bounded-isolated-group-v1` or `bounded-nested-isolated-group-v1` with exact direct-child/nested/order/depth/mask-coverage evidence. Exact settings, stack order, source/approximation models, mask coverage, crossed group indices, group bounds, and baked state are shared by the File Inspector and the existing strict inspect/extract MCP workflow. Backdrop-independent pass-through groups recursively flatten through `bounded-pass-through-flatten-v1` with exact indices and explicit external-backdrop blockers. TySh semantic interpretation, embedded-raster provenance, bounded uniform-style project-font rerasterization, and PlLd/SoLd/SoLE smart-object placement plus embedded-preview provenance are complete. Remaining work begins with smart-object linked/embedded payload handling and live rendering, rich multi-run/complex-script/vertical/warped text, backdrop-dependent pass-through semantics, proprietary/profile/external-only LUTs, PSB/high-depth modes, and other layered fidelity.

Model-material-remap correction (#397): model importers persist a bounded exact-name replacement table from imported source materials to contained project `.material` assets. Shared execution discovers source material objects, mesh/submesh references, and texture counts; replaces direct and MultiMaterial assignments; reports missing sources/replacements; and publishes the same serialized result through live editor import, Inspector artifacts, editor builds, and CLI builds. Replacement assets and indexed dependencies participate in exact fingerprints and build-cache invalidation. The Model Inspector authors and validates the table, while `get_model_material_remaps` and `set_model_material_remaps` provide strict complete-table workflows to Codex CLI and Claude CLI. Later corrections add generated LODs plus Unity-style material search and material/PNG/JPEG texture extraction; additional embedded codecs remain incomplete, so overall Model Importer and Asset Pipeline parity remains Partial.

Generated-model-LOD correction (#398): model importers persist up to eight closed retained-quality/distance definitions with strictly increasing centimeter distances and decreasing quality. Shared execution runs bounded quadratic-error simplification, preserves parent/local transforms and material assignments, records actual geometry reductions, and atomically rolls generated output back on failure. Generated meshes and source `lodMeshIds`/`lodDistances` serialize into Inspector artifacts, editor builds, and CLI builds and reconstruct through Babylon's runtime loader. The specialized Model Inspector authors the table and displays per-source evidence; `get_model_generated_lods` and `set_model_generated_lods` use strict exact leases for Codex CLI and Claude CLI. The later #403 correction extends this originally static-only path to skinned and morph-target geometry with exact deformation reconstruction; mobile/console-specific profiles and additional embedded codecs remain incomplete, so overall Model Importer and Asset Pipeline parity remains Partial.

Model-material-search correction (#399): model importers persist Unity-style naming by source material, base texture with source fallback, or model-plus-material and search through no scope, the adjacent `Materials` folder, nearest-first parent `Materials` folders, or every project `.material` asset. The shared bounded planner searches at most 10,000 material assets, prefers exact case before case-insensitive compatibility, chooses only a unique best-rank match, records every ordered candidate for ambiguity diagnosis, and never overrides an explicit remap. Inspector artifacts, editor builds, and CLI builds load the same matched material and preserve direct/MultiMaterial behavior. Exact editor fingerprints hash the complete active scope; CLI runtime dependency fingerprints rescan the current scope so matching files that appear after a cached build trigger reprocessing. The Model Inspector displays naming, scope, searched count, candidate name, unique match, and ambiguity. `get_model_material_search` and `set_model_material_search` expose strict exact-leased workflows to Codex CLI and Claude CLI. Later corrections add material/PNG/JPEG texture extraction, Web/Desktop overrides, and deformation-safe generated LODs; additional codecs and mobile/console-specific profiles remain incomplete, so overall Model Importer and Asset Pipeline parity remains Partial.

Model-material-extraction correction (#400): raw embedded model materials can now become ordinary editable project `.material` assets through the Model Inspector or strict MCP. A shared planner bounds the source count, requires unambiguous exact source names, generates portable filenames, canonicalizes JSON while removing volatile runtime IDs, and records create/reuse/conflict decisions under a source/dependency/destination fingerprint. Apply never overwrites; equivalent destinations are reused, changed destinations block, newly created files are atomically published, and any failure restores importer metadata and removes newly created assets. Successful extraction persists exact remaps, refreshes the registry, and returns the new model-importer fingerprint used by editor preview, editor builds, and CLI builds. `inspect_model_material_extraction` and `extract_model_materials` expose the same operation to Codex CLI and Claude CLI. The later #401/#402/#403 corrections add PNG/JPEG texture extraction, Web/Desktop overrides, and deformation-safe generated LODs; additional codecs and mobile/console-specific profiles remain incomplete, so overall Model Importer and Asset Pipeline parity remains Partial.

Model-texture-extraction correction (#401): exact-remapped editable model materials can now publish embedded PNG/JPEG bytes as normal project texture assets. The shared material scanner recognizes Babylon `base64String` in addition to embedded `url`/`name`, retains a human filename hint, and removes the embedded field during rewrite. A bounded planner validates MIME syntax plus real PNG/JPEG signatures, caps each image at 32 MiB, all images at 256 MiB, and unique images at 128, deduplicates identical bytes across materials, and records create/reuse/conflict decisions. Apply requires an exact source/settings/material/destination fingerprint, never overwrites a conflicting texture, transactionally backs up every rewritten material, atomically publishes images and material documents, and rolls everything back on failure. `inspect_model_texture_extraction` and `extract_model_textures` expose the workflow to Codex CLI and Claude CLI. The later #402/#403 corrections add Web/Desktop overrides and deformation-safe generated LODs; additional embedded codecs and mobile/console-specific profiles remain incomplete, so overall Model Importer and Asset Pipeline parity remains Partial.

Model-platform-override correction (#402): each model importer now persists closed Web and Desktop profiles that can independently inherit or replace target-sensitive Default processing. Effective settings cover scale/unit conversion, material/texture/animation inclusion, animation type, Optimize Game Object, colliders, mesh compression/optimization/welding, normals/tangents, and generated LODs. The specialized Model Inspector authors the complete supported surface. Web and Electron build profiles select Web and Desktop respectively, standalone CLI packing accepts `--model-platform`, both export implementations put the selected target in their cache key, and portable result evidence distinguishes base from effective settings. Exact leases invalidate whenever any target map changes. `get_model_platform_overrides` and `set_model_platform_overrides` use closed nested schemas shared by Codex CLI and Claude CLI. The later #403 correction makes target-selected generated LODs deformation-safe; mobile/console-specific profiles and additional embedded codecs remain incomplete, so overall Model Importer and Asset Pipeline parity remains Partial.

Deformation-safe generated-model-LOD correction (#403): the shared quadratic-error path now uses an isolated geometry clone with an RGBA-encoded 32-bit source-vertex identity, so reduction never overwrites the imported source and every surviving output vertex has an exact provenance record. All original vertex streams are remapped, including normals, tangents, UV0–UV6, colors, and paired four/eight-influence bone index/weight buffers. Generated skinned meshes retain their source skeleton and influencer count. Each generated morph manager receives independently sized targets: position deltas are applied to the reduced base while target normals, tangents, UVs, UV2s, and colors map by provenance. Direct target animations and AnimationGroup influence tracks are cloned; serialized source metadata reconnects arbitrary source influence changes after scene/container parsing and model instantiation. Invalid streams, mismatched morph targets, and partial multi-mesh generation roll back all LOD links, generated meshes, managers, observers, and added tracks. The Inspector and existing strict leased LOD MCP tools expose deformation mode, skin streams, exact mapped-vertex counts, preserved streams, morph targets, and track counts. Focused shared/editor/CLI tests load real serialized skinned+morph Babylon models, build them, reload the outputs, and verify live influence synchronization. The later #404 correction adds artist-authored imported-mesh LOD groups; bone/morph-aware error metrics and additional reduction-quality controls remain broader parity work, so overall Model Importer and Asset Pipeline parity remains Partial.

Artist-authored model-LOD correction (#404): model importers persist up to 128 exact groups containing one LOD0 source mesh and up to eight existing lower-detail imported meshes with strictly increasing transition distances. Closed normalization rejects duplicate mesh use, unknown fields, invalid bounds, missing names, and ambiguous imported names before mutation. The shared application path validates the complete table, attaches Babylon LOD links atomically, hides and disables collisions on lower levels, preserves authored geometry/material/skin/morph content, excludes assigned meshes from generated simplification, and rolls metadata, visibility, collisions, and links back on failure. The Model Inspector provides exact mesh selectors, add/remove controls, distance editing, deterministic `Name_LOD#` detection, and applied geometry/deformation evidence. Editor artifacts and CLI builds serialize the same `lodMeshIds`/`lodDistances`, and fresh Babylon runtime loads reconstruct switching. `get_model_authored_lods` and `set_model_authored_lods` expose the exact-leased workflow to Codex CLI and Claude CLI. Bone/morph-aware simplification error metrics and additional reduction controls remain incomplete, so overall Model Importer and Asset Pipeline parity remains Partial.

Live prefab-structure correction (#357, complete): one live instance can be compared non-mutatively against an exact outer or nested source boundary. The result derives removals, transform/empty/clone/serialized-mesh/nested-prefab additions, reparents, transform differences, deterministic signatures, a complete proposed structural override set, and explicit blockers. Exact-revision capture writes structure only into variants, bounds serialized mesh payloads to 2 MiB with resource-count limits, updates captured live provenance after the asset write succeeds, retains ordered outer-to-inner links for separately instantiated nested prefabs, rejects stale leases without mutating nodes, and returns promptly even when the editor window is hidden. The Mesh Inspector detects and captures live structure and provides searchable multi-instance signature comparison. Three shared MCP tools expose inspection, capture, and comparison to Codex CLI and Claude CLI. Focused prefab coverage passes 17 tests, the complete editor gate passes 408 tests across 118 files, all production builds and focused lint pass, stdio discovery reports all three schemas among 705 tools, and a live editor scenario captured and re-instantiated both a newly authored box and a separately instantiated two-node nested prefab with preserved geometry and source-link provenance. Broader Scene and Prefab parity remain Partial where the matrix identifies Unity-depth gaps beyond this correction.

Multi-instance Prefab Overrides correction (#409, complete): one bounded snapshot now compares 1–64 explicit live boundaries, every boundary for one prefab path, or every outer live prefab instance. Exact per-target revisions/fingerprints and one deterministic batch fingerprint lease up to 8192 flattened paginated rows. The shared editor window groups targets, preserves structural selection per target, searches/filters rows, exposes Atomic versus Best effort behavior, and reports partial per-target failures. Atomic Apply acquires every involved source lane, rejects duplicate or dependency-related sources, backs up every source and captured live provenance, and restores all of them on any failure. Atomic Revert restores non-overlapping transform/visibility/component values across targets and rolls all live values back on failure; cross-instance structural Revert requires explicit best-effort semantics. Four strict shared MCP tools provide inspection, Apply, Revert, and UI opening to Codex CLI and Claude CLI. Focused changed-suite coverage passes 16 tests; complete gates pass 237 tools and 571 editor tests; all production builds and focused lint pass; stdio discovers 816 closed schemas with pre-editor validation; and a live Electron scenario proves two-source atomic Apply, two-target atomic Revert, visible grouped UI evidence, and complete temporary node/asset cleanup. Prefab parity remains Partial only for specialized/arbitrary Inspector adornments, visual merge/conflict resolution, and large-team review/approval ergonomics.

Visual Prefab merge correction (#411, complete): every recursive variant conflict retains the exact authored override collection/index and owner path that generated it. Prefab Stage replaces blanket override deletion with a three-column current-base/authored-variant/resolved-or-live comparison, safe per-row Use Base/Keep Variant/Retarget choices, exact unique node/parent/property/clone-source selectors, inherited-owner evidence, and one complete confirmation-gated apply. Mutation verifies exact variant/base revisions and an optional live fingerprint, rejects partial/stale/ambiguous/unsafe plans before publication, supports lossless component-conflict conversion, and restores the original raw variant if post-publish validation fails. `inspect_prefab_variant_conflicts` and `resolve_prefab_variant_conflicts` expose the identical bounded workflow to Codex CLI and Claude CLI. Focused Prefab coverage passes 47 tests; complete gates pass 237 tools and 579 editor tests; full production builds and affected lint pass; stdio discovers both new closed schemas among 818 tools; and a live Electron scenario visibly resolves a temporary stale variant and re-inspects zero conflicts/overrides with complete fixture cleanup. Prefab parity remains Partial only for large-team review, ownership, and approval ergonomics.

Large-team Prefab review correction (#412, complete): a contained atomic version-1 store under `.babylon-editor` persists stable review/path, owner/reviewer, comment, decision, request-round, and exact recursively resolved revision evidence separately from runtime Prefab assets. Collaboration enforcement resolves authoritative member identities and roles, while local workflows accept explicit stable actors. Owners/admins manage requests, only assigned reviewers decide, exact fingerprints prevent concurrent metadata loss, and any Prefab/base/variant revision change automatically stales approval until a new round. Prefab Stage exposes the complete queue item workflow and stale warning. Five strict shared MCP tools expose bounded list/inspect/set/comment/decision operations. Focused coverage passes 4 tests; complete gates pass 237 tools and 583 editor tests; editor/MCP builds, focused lint, and formatting pass; stdio discovers 823 closed tools and rejects unknown fields with `-32602`; and live Electron visibly proves request, comment, approval, automatic staleness, and complete temporary cleanup. The dedicated Prefabs row is now Complete for its Unity matrix scope.

HDR/OpenEXR texture-importer correction (#422, complete): one shared bounded linear-float backend now decodes Radiance RGBE and scanline OpenEXR, preserves high-dynamic output, produces deterministic HDR/EXR mips, optional RGBA32F data, ACES previews, exact range/non-finite diagnostics, six cube-face previews for valid 2:1 panoramas, and an HDR environment derivative for EXR builds. The Assets Browser, File Inspector, drag/drop, material/environment assignment, editor/CLI build paths, runtime manifests, scene serialization, and exported runtime share the same artifacts. Existing texture inspect/apply and material tools plus image conversion and environment assignment expose closed annotated MCP contracts to Codex CLI and Claude CLI. Complete suites pass 256 tools, 614 editor, and 27 CLI tests; affected production builds and formatting pass; stdio discovers 832 tools and rejects unknown fields with `-32602`; and a live Electron scenario imports HDR and EXR, verifies exact float/range/cubemap evidence, assigns EXR image-based lighting, captures a rendered scene, restores the original environment, removes all fixtures/artifacts, and reports no terminal exception. Texture/Image parity remains Partial for platform profiles, rotated packing, layered PSD extraction, and dependency-aware reimport.

For each **Missing** or **Partial** row, work in this order:

1. Design the editor data model and persistence/export format.
2. Build the editor UI and runtime behavior.
3. Add MCP read and mutation tools using the same implementation path.
4. Run a live port-3712 scenario and record the result in `MCP-DEVELOPMENT.md`.
5. Mark the row complete only after formatting, workspace builds, and live verification pass.

## Initial delivery order

1. Prefabs and the asset database foundation.
2. ProBuilder-equivalent editable mesh operations.
3. Terrain assets and brushes.
4. Splines and 2D tilemap workflow.
5. Animator, rigging/IK, virtual cameras, and audio mixer.
6. Shader/VFX graph diagnostics and lighting workflow.
7. Physics expansion including cloth/2D physics and navigation agents.
8. Build profiles, testing/profiling, packages/collaboration, and platform/service tracks.
